In the case of
ii)
, note that the fields

\widetilde{h}_{ij}:=\Psi^{-2}h_{ij}\,,\qquad\widetilde{K}_{ij}:=\frac{1}{|\Psi|}\left(K_{ij}+\varepsilon\frac{z}{\Psi}h_{ij}\right)\,,

are gauge invariants, meaning that they are left invariant by the gauge transformations of the (CVC) described in
4.12
.
∎

Proof.

i)
Let
(\widetilde{\mathcal{S}},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{K})
be a solution to the (VC),
\Psi
be a smooth nowhere vanishing function,
\kappa
be a smooth covector field and
z
be a smooth scalar field on
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
. By definition of the fields
K
,
Q
,
E
,
M
and
T
through equations (
4.22
), the 10-tuple written in the proposition belongs in
\mathscr{D}
and (
CVC.3
), (
CVC.5
), (
CVC.8
), (
CVC.9
) are directly verified. Let us prove that the other equations of the (CVC) also hold.

•

Equation (
CVC.11
): one deduces from (
4.22a
) that

(\widetilde{K}_{i}{}^{i})^{2}-\widetilde{K}_{ij}\widetilde{K}^{ij}=\Psi^{2}\left((K_{i}{}^{i})^{2}-K_{ij}K^{ij}\right)+4\varepsilon z\Psi K_{i}{}^{i}+6z^{2}\,.

Furthermore, one can derive from (
4.12
) that

\widetilde{r}=4\widetilde{l}=12\left(\widehat{s}_{h}\Psi-\frac{1}{2}h^{-1}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h},\widehat{\zeta}_{h})\right)\,.

The result follows from (
VC.1
).

•

Equation (
CVC.10
): by differentiating (
CVC.11
), one has

\widehat{\sigma}(d\Psi)_{i}+\Psi(d\widehat{\sigma})_{i}=\varepsilon z(dz)_{i}+\left(\widehat{D}_{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}+\kappa_{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}\right)h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}\,.

Using (
4.22b
), (
4.22d
) and by definition of
\widehat{\zeta}_{h}
, one finds

\Psi\left((d\widehat{\sigma})_{i}-\kappa_{i}\widehat{\sigma}+\varepsilon zT_{i}+Q_{ij}h^{jk}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}\right)=0\,.

•

Equation (
CVC.4
): one can write (
VC.2
) under the form

2\widetilde{h}^{ik}\widetilde{D}_{[j}\widetilde{K}_{k]i}=0\,.

Yet using (
4.22a
),

\displaystyle\sign(\Psi)\widetilde{D}_{[j}\widetilde{K}_{k]i}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{D}_{[j}\left(\Psi^{-1}K_{k]i}+\varepsilon z\widetilde{h}_{k]i}\right)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\widetilde{D}_{[j}K_{k]i}-\Psi^{-2}(d\Psi)_{[j}K_{k]i}-\varepsilon\widetilde{h}_{i[j}(dz)_{k]}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{k]i}+S_{i[j}{}^{lm}K_{k]l}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{m}\Psi^{-1}\right)-\Psi^{-2}(d\Psi)_{[j}K_{k]i}-\varepsilon\widetilde{h}_{i[j}(dz)_{k]}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{k]i}-\Psi^{-2}\left(-(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{[j}K_{k]i}+h_{i[j}K_{k]}{}^{l}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}+(d\Psi)_{[j}K_{k]i}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\widetilde{h}_{i[j}(dz)_{k]}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{k]i}+\kappa_{[j}K_{k]i}\right)+\varepsilon\widetilde{h}_{i[j}\left(-(dz)_{k]}+\varepsilon K_{k]}{}^{l}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}\right)\,.

By taking the trace and with equations (
4.22d
) and (
2.54
), one deduces that

0=2\widetilde{h}^{ik}\widetilde{D}_{[j}\widetilde{K}_{k]i}=|\Psi|\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon h^{ik}G_{jki}(K,\kappa)-2T_{j}\right)\,.

Hence the result. Note that by combining (
4.22e
) and (
CVC.4
), one has now

G_{ijk}(K,\kappa)=\Psi M_{ijk}-2\varepsilon h_{k[i}T_{j]}\,.

(4.23)

•

Equation (
CVC.7
): from (
4.22d
) and (
4.22b
), one has

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{i}T_{j}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\Big(-(d\Psi)_{i}T_{j}-\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{D}_{j}z-\varepsilon\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K_{jk}+\kappa_{i}K_{jk}\right)h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}+\varepsilon K_{j}{}^{l}\left(-\widehat{D}_{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}-\kappa_{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\Big(-(d\Psi)_{i}T_{j}-\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{D}_{j}z-\varepsilon\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K_{jk}+\kappa_{i}K_{jk}\right)h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}+\varepsilon K_{j}{}^{l}\left(\Psi Q_{il}-zK_{il}-\widehat{\sigma}h_{il}\right)\Big)\,.

Hence

2\widehat{D}_{[i}T_{j]}=\Psi^{-1}\left(-2(d\Psi)_{[i}T_{j]}-\varepsilon G_{ijk}h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}-2\varepsilon\Psi K^{l}{}_{[i}Q_{j]l}\right)\,.

By injecting (
4.23
), one has

\displaystyle 2\widehat{D}_{[i}T_{j]}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(-2(d\Psi)_{[i}T_{j]}-\varepsilon\Psi M_{ijk}h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}+2(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{[i}T_{j]}-2\varepsilon\Psi K^{l}{}_{[i}Q_{j]l}\right)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(-\varepsilon\Psi M_{ijk}h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}+2\Psi\kappa_{[i}T_{j]}-2\varepsilon\Psi K^{l}{}_{[i}Q_{j]l}\right)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon M_{ijk}h^{kl}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}+2\kappa_{[i}T_{j]}-2\varepsilon K^{l}{}_{[i}Q_{j]l}\,.

•

Equation (
CVC.6
): from (
4.22b
), one has

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{i}Q_{jk}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\Big(-(d\Psi)_{i}Q_{jk}-\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{D}_{j}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}-(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}\widehat{D}_{i}\kappa_{j}-\kappa_{j}\widehat{D}_{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+(dz)_{i}K_{jk}+z\widehat{D}_{i}K_{jk}+\left((d\widehat{\sigma})_{i}-2\kappa_{i}\widehat{\sigma}\right)h_{jk}\Big)\,.

By using (
4.22b
), (
4.22d
) and (
CVC.10
), one deduces that

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{i}Q_{jk}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\Big(-(d\Psi)_{i}Q_{jk}-\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{D}_{j}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}-(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}\widehat{D}_{i}\kappa_{j}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\kappa_{j}\left(\kappa_{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}-zK_{ik}-\widehat{\sigma}h_{ik}+\Psi Q_{ik}\right)-\Psi T_{i}K_{jk}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\varepsilon K_{i}{}^{l}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}K_{jk}+z\widehat{D}_{i}K_{jk}-\widehat{\sigma}\kappa_{i}h_{jk}-\varepsilon zT_{i}h_{jk}-h_{jk}Q_{il}h^{lm}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{m}\Big)\,.

Hence

\displaystyle 2\widehat{D}_{[i}Q_{j]k}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\Big(-2(d\Psi)_{[i}Q_{j]k}-2\widehat{D}_{[i}\widehat{D}_{j]}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}-2(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}\widehat{D}_{[i}\kappa_{j]}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+2z\kappa_{[i}K_{j]k}-2\Psi\kappa_{[i}Q_{j]k}-2\Psi T_{[i}K_{j]k}-2\varepsilon K_{[i}{}^{l}K_{j]k}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+2z\widehat{D}_{[i}K_{j]k}-2\varepsilon zT_{[i}h_{j]k}+2h_{k[i}Q_{j]l}h^{lm}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{m}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\Big(-2\widehat{D}_{[i}\widehat{D}_{j]}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}-2(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{[i}Q_{j]k}-2(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}\widehat{D}_{[i}\kappa_{j]}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+2h_{k[i}Q_{j]l}h^{lm}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{m}-2\varepsilon K_{[i}{}^{l}K_{j]k}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}-2\Psi T_{[i}K_{j]k}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+z\left(2\widehat{D}_{[i}K_{j]k}+2\kappa_{[i}K_{j]k}+2\varepsilon h_{k[i}T_{j]}\right)\Big)\,.

With (
4.22b
) and (
4.23
), it follows that

\displaystyle 2\widehat{D}_{[i}Q_{j]k}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(-2\widehat{D}_{[i}\widehat{D}_{j]}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}-2S_{k[i}{}^{lm}Q_{j]m}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}-2\varepsilon K_{[i}{}^{l}K_{j]k}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}\right)+2K_{k[i}T_{j]}+zM_{ijk}\,.

One has

-2\widehat{D}_{[i}\widehat{D}_{j]}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{k}=\widehat{r}^{l}{}_{kij}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}=2S_{k[i}{}^{lm}\widehat{l}_{j]m}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}\,,

and, since Weyl candidates vanish identically in dimension
3
(see also (
2.53b
)),

2K_{[i}{}^{l}K_{j]k}=2S_{k[i}{}^{lm}F_{j]m}(K)\,.

One concludes with (
4.22c
).

•

Equation (
CVC.1
): from (
4.23
), one has

\widehat{D}_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}=\Psi^{-1}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}+\widehat{D}_{i}G_{jk}{}^{i}(K,\kappa)+2\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{[j}T_{k]}\right)\,.

By definition of
G_{ijk}(K,\kappa)
, see (
2.54
) and using (
CVC.4
), one has

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{i}G_{jk}{}^{i}(K,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{i}\left(h^{il}\left(2\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{k]l}+2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]l}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{i}\left(2\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}-2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{D}_{j}K_{k}{}^{i}-\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{D}_{k}K_{j}{}^{i}-\widehat{D}_{i}\left(2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{j}\widehat{D}_{i}K_{k}{}^{i}-\widehat{r}^{l}{}_{kij}K_{l}{}^{i}+\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{lij}K_{k}{}^{l}-\widehat{D}_{k}\widehat{D}_{i}K_{j}{}^{i}+\widehat{r}^{l}{}_{jik}K_{l}{}^{i}-\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{lik}K_{j}{}^{l}-\widehat{D}_{i}\left(2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{j}\widehat{D}_{i}K_{k}{}^{i}-\widehat{D}_{k}\widehat{D}_{i}K_{j}{}^{i}+\widehat{r}^{l}{}_{ijk}K_{l}{}^{i}+2\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{li[j}K_{k]}{}^{l}-\widehat{D}_{i}\left(2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{j}\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K_{k}{}^{i}-\kappa_{i}K_{k}{}^{i}-\widehat{D}_{k}K+\kappa_{k}K\right)+\widehat{D}_{j}\left(\kappa_{i}K_{k}{}^{i}-\kappa_{k}K\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\widehat{D}_{k}\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K_{j}{}^{i}-\kappa_{i}K_{j}{}^{i}-\widehat{D}_{j}K+\kappa_{j}K\right)-\widehat{D}_{k}\left(\kappa_{i}K_{j}{}^{i}-\kappa_{j}K\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\widehat{r}^{l}{}_{ijk}K_{l}{}^{i}+2\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{li[j}K_{k]}{}^{l}-\widehat{D}_{i}\left(2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=-2\widehat{D}_{[j}\left(h^{il}G_{k]il}(K,\kappa)\right)+\widehat{r}_{(il)jk}K^{il}+2\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{li[j}K_{k]}{}^{l}+\kappa_{i}\left(2\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}-2\kappa_{[j}K_{k]}{}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{D}_{k]}\kappa_{i}+2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{D}_{|i|}\kappa_{k]}-2K\widehat{D}_{[j}\kappa_{k]}-2\kappa_{[j}\left(\widehat{D}_{|i|}K_{k]}{}^{i}-K_{k]}{}^{i}\kappa_{i}-\widehat{D}_{k]}K\right)

\displaystyle=-4\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{[j}T_{k]}+\widehat{r}_{(il)jk}K^{il}+2\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{li[j}K_{k]}{}^{l}+\kappa_{i}G_{jk}{}^{i}(K,\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad-2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{D}_{k]}\kappa_{i}+2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{D}_{|i|}\kappa_{k]}-2K\widehat{D}_{[j}\kappa_{k]}+4\varepsilon\kappa_{[j}T_{k]}\,.

One deduces from (
2.19b
) and (
2.19c
) that

\widehat{r}_{(il)jk}K^{il}=2g_{il}K^{il}\widehat{D}_{[j}\kappa_{k]}=2K\widehat{D}_{[j}\kappa_{k]}\,,

and from (
2.22
) and (
2.24a
) that

\widehat{r}^{i}{}_{lij}=2S_{l[i}{}^{im}\widehat{l}_{j]m}=\widehat{l}_{jl}-\widehat{D}_{[j}\kappa_{l]}+g_{jl}\widehat{l}_{i}{}^{i}\,.

It follows that

\widehat{D}_{i}G_{jk}{}^{i}(K,\kappa)=-4\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{[j}T_{k]}+\kappa_{i}G_{jk}{}^{i}(K,\kappa)-2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{l}_{k]i}+4\varepsilon\kappa_{[j}T_{k]}\,,

and thus

\widehat{D}_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}=\Psi^{-1}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}+\kappa_{i}G_{jk}{}^{i}(K,\kappa)-2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{l}_{k]i}-2\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{[j}T_{k]}+4\varepsilon\kappa_{[j}T_{k]}\right)\,.

Using (
4.23
) and (
CVC.7
), one obtains

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}+\Psi\kappa_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}-2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{l}_{k]i}-\varepsilon\left(2\widehat{D}_{[j}T_{k]}-2\kappa_{[j}T_{k]}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}+\Psi\kappa_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}-2K^{i}{}_{[j}\widehat{l}_{k]i}+K^{i}{}_{[j}Q_{k]i}+M_{jk}{}^{i}(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{i}M_{jk}{}^{i}+\Psi^{-1}2K^{i}{}_{[j}\left(Q_{k]i}-\widehat{l}_{k]i}\right)\,.

From the definition of
F_{ij}(K)
, see (
2.54
), one deduces that
K^{i}{}_{[j}F_{k]i}(K)=0
. One concludes using (
4.22c
).

•

Equation (
CVC.2
): from (
4.22c
) one has

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{i}E_{j}{}^{i}

\displaystyle=h^{ik}\widehat{D}_{i}E_{jk}+2\kappa_{i}E_{j}{}^{i}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}h^{ik}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}E_{jk}+\widehat{D}_{i}Q_{jk}-\widehat{D}_{i}\widehat{l}_{jk}+\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{i}F_{jk}(K)\right)+2\kappa_{i}E_{j}{}^{i}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}h^{ik}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}E_{jk}+2\widehat{D}_{[i}Q_{j]k}-\widehat{c}_{ijk}+\widehat{D}_{j}\left(\Psi E_{ik}-\varepsilon F_{ik}(K)\right)+\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{i}F_{jk}(K)\right)+2\kappa_{i}E_{j}{}^{i}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{-1}h^{ik}\left(-(d\Psi)_{i}E_{jk}+2\widehat{D}_{[i}Q_{j]k}-\widehat{c}_{ijk}+2\varepsilon\widehat{D}_{[i}F_{j]k}(K)\right)+2\kappa_{i}E_{j}{}^{i}\,.

Using (
CVC.6
) and (
2.26a
), one has

\displaystyle h^{ik}\left(2\widehat{D}_{[i}Q_{j]k}-\widehat{c}_{ijk}\right)

\displaystyle=h^{ik}\left(2K_{k[i}T_{j]}+zM_{ijk}-2(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{l}S_{k[i}{}^{lm}E_{j]m}\right)

\displaystyle=2h^{ik}K_{k[i}T_{j]}+(\widehat{\zeta}_{h})_{i}E_{j}{}^{i}\,.

Thus, it remains to prove that

K^{ik}M_{jki}=\Psi^{-1}h^{ik}\left(2\varepsilon K_{k[i}T_{j]}+2\widehat{D}_{[i}F_{j]k}(K)\right)\,.

With (
4.23
), this is equivalent to

K^{ik}G_{jki}(K,\kappa)+4\varepsilon K^{k}{}_{[j}T_{k]}=2h^{ik}\widehat{D}_{[i}F_{j]k}(K)\,.

To prove the above equality, define

P_{ij}:=KK_{ij}-K_{ik}K^{k}{}_{j}\,,\qquad P:=h^{ij}P_{ij}=K^{2}-K_{ij}K^{ij}\,.

By definition of
F_{ij}(K)
, see (
2.54
), one has
F_{ij}(K)=P_{ij}-Ph_{ij}/4
. Thus

\displaystyle 2h^{ik}\widehat{D}_{[i}F_{j]k}(K)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{i}F^{i}{}_{j}(K)-2\kappa_{i}F^{i}{}_{j}(K)-\widehat{D}_{j}F^{i}{}_{i}(K)+2\kappa_{j}F^{i}{}_{i}(K)

\displaystyle=\widehat{D}_{i}P^{i}{}_{j}-2\kappa_{i}P^{i}{}_{j}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\widehat{D}_{j}P-2\kappa_{j}P\right)

\displaystyle=\left(K\widehat{D}_{i}K^{i}{}_{j}+K_{j}{}^{i}\widehat{D}_{i}K-K^{ik}\widehat{D}_{i}K_{kj}-K_{j}{}^{k}\widehat{D}_{i}K^{i}{}_{k}-2\kappa_{i}K_{kj}K^{ki}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-2\kappa_{i}\left(KK^{i}{}_{j}-K^{ik}K_{kj}\right)-\left(K\widehat{D}_{j}K-K^{ik}\widehat{D}_{j}K_{ik}-2\kappa_{j}K^{ik}K_{ik}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{j}\left(K^{2}-K_{ik}K^{ik}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(K_{j}{}^{i}-K\delta_{j}{}^{i}\right)\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K-\widehat{D}_{k}K^{k}{}_{i}\right)+2K^{ik}\widehat{D}_{[j}K_{i]k}

\displaystyle\quad-2K\kappa_{i}K^{i}{}_{j}+\kappa_{j}K^{ik}K_{ik}+\kappa_{j}K^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(K_{j}{}^{i}-K\delta_{j}{}^{i}\right)\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K-\widehat{D}_{k}K^{k}{}_{i}\right)+K^{ik}G_{jik}(K,\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad-2K\kappa_{i}K^{i}{}_{j}+\kappa_{i}K^{ik}K_{jk}+\kappa_{j}K^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(K_{j}{}^{i}-K\delta_{j}{}^{i}\right)\left(\widehat{D}_{i}K-\kappa_{i}K-\widehat{D}_{k}K^{k}{}_{i}+\kappa_{k}K^{k}{}_{i}\right)+K^{ik}G_{jik}(K,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\left(K_{j}{}^{i}-K\delta_{j}{}^{i}\right)h^{kl}G_{ikl}(K,\kappa)+K^{ik}G_{jik}(K,\kappa)

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\left(K_{j}{}^{i}-K\delta_{j}{}^{i}\right)T_{i}+K^{ik}G_{jik}(K,\kappa)

\displaystyle=4\varepsilon K^{i}{}_{[j}T_{i]}+K^{ik}G_{jik}(K,\kappa)\,,

where (
CVC.4
) has been used to obtain the penultimate equality.

ii)
Let
(\mathcal{S},h,E,M,\Psi,\kappa,K,z,T,Q)\in\mathscr{D}
be a solution to the (CVC) and let
\mathcal{U}
be a set where
\Psi
nowhere vanishes. The 10-tuple
(\mathcal{U},h,E,M,\Psi,\kappa,K,z,T,Q)\in\mathscr{D}
is a fortiori solution to the (CVC). Let use the gauge freedoms of
4.12
to modify it. By applying first
\Weylchg_{\chi}^{\perp}
with
\chi:=-(z/\Psi)|_{\mathcal{U}}
, then
\Weylchg_{\omega}^{\parallel}
with
\omega:=-(\kappa+d\ln|\Psi|)|_{\mathcal{U}}
and finally
\Conf_{\phi}
with the conformal factor
\Omega:=|\Psi|^{-1}|_{\mathcal{U}}
, one obtains a gauge-equivalent 10-tuple
(\mathcal{U},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{E},\widetilde{M},\widetilde{\Psi},\widetilde{\kappa},\widetilde{K},\widetilde{z},\widetilde{T},\widetilde{Q})\in\mathscr{D}
, thus also solution to the (CVC), such that
\widetilde{h}=\Psi^{-2}h|_{\mathcal{U}}
,
\widetilde{\Psi}=\pm 1
,
\widetilde{\kappa}=0
,
\widetilde{K}=|\Psi|^{-1}(K+\varepsilon z\Psi^{-1}h)|_{\mathcal{U}}
and
\widetilde{z}=0
. One deduces that
\widetilde{T}=0
from (
CVC.9
). Finally, equations (
CVC.11
) and (
CVC.4
) resume to the (VC) on the triple
(\mathcal{U},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{K})
.
∎

4.1.7
Application to 4-dimensional aAdS spaces

We would like to end this presentation of the general framework of the (CVE) by emphasizing its application for 4-dimensional aAdS spaces and the links between the conformal point of view and the FG-type asymptotic expansions near the conformal boundary described in
Section

3.2
.

A straightforward extension of
4.20
gives the following proposition.

Proposition 4.22
.

i)

Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathfrak{I},\widetilde{g})
be a 4-dimensional aAdS space, in the sense of
3.1
, with smooth rescaled metrics. If
(\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathfrak{I},\widetilde{g})
is solution to the (
VE
) – for
\Lambda=-3
necessarily – then for any boundary defining function
x
of
\mathfrak{I}
and any smooth covector field
\kappa
on
\mathcal{M}
, the rescaled Weyl tensor
x^{-1}\widetilde{W}
extends smoothly to
\mathfrak{I}
and the quintuple
(\mathcal{M},g:=x^{2}\widetilde{g},x^{-1}W,x,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
is solution to the (
4.1
) with
\Lambda=-3
.

Moreover, the conformal boundary
\mathscr{I}
, as defined in
4.15
, coincide with the conformal boundary
\mathfrak{I}
of the aAdS space, hence the common name.

ii)

Conversely, let
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
be a quintuple with non-empty conformal boundary
\mathscr{I}
. If it is solution to the (
4.1
) for
\Lambda=-3
then for any closure
\mathcal{U}
of a connected component of
\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathscr{I}
, the triple
(\mathcal{U},\mathfrak{I}:=\mathscr{I}\cap\mathcal{U},\widetilde{g}:=|\Theta|^{-2}g)
is a 4-dimensional aAdS space with smooth rescaled metrics and
(\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathfrak{I},\widetilde{g})
is solution to the (
VE
) – for
\Lambda=-3
necessarily.

Proof.

i)

By
i)
of
4.20
, the quintuplet
(\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathfrak{I},g,x^{-1}W,x,\kappa)
is solution to the (CVE) with
\Lambda=-3
. The rescaled metric
g:=x^{2}\widetilde{g}
extends smoothly to
\mathfrak{I}
by assumption. The rescaled Weyl tensor
x^{-1}W
extends smoothly on
\mathfrak{I}
thanks to
2.11
and the asymptotic expansions of
3.13
(with
n=3
). Hence the quintuplet
(\mathcal{M},g,x^{-1}W,x,\kappa)
is in
\mathscr{E}
and, by a continuity argument, is solution to the (CVE) with
\Lambda=-3
. Since
x
is a boundary defining function of
\mathfrak{I}
, one has
\mathscr{I}=\mathfrak{I}
.

ii)

By
ii)
of
4.20
,
(\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathfrak{I},\widetilde{g})
is solution to the (VE) for
\Lambda=-3
. By definition of
\mathcal{U}
,
|\Theta|
is positive on
\mathcal{U}
. With
4.16
, one deduces that
|\Theta|
is a boundary defining function of
\mathscr{I}\cap\mathcal{U}
and that
(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{I}\cap\mathcal{U},|\Theta|^{-2}g)
is aAdS. ∎

Note that the umbilical character (see
2.59
) of connected components of the conformal boundary has been proven by two different methods in dimension 4. First,
4.17
states that the extrinsic curvature of a connected component of the conformal boundary for the Weyl connection
\widehat{\nabla}
is given by

(\widehat{\mathfrak{K}}_{g})_{ij}=-\frac{\widehat{s}_{g}|_{\mathfrak{S}}}{\mathfrak{z}}\mathfrak{h}_{ij}\,.

(
4.16b
)

This was obtained using the framework of the (CVE), which is only applicable in dimension 4. Secondly, the connected components of the conformal boundary are totally geodesic in all dimensions for the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
of a rescaled metric
g=x^{2}\widetilde{g}
with
x
a boundary defining function verifying (
3.4
), as has been deduced from the FG expansion in
Section

3.2.2
. In particular, this implies that the connected components are umbilical by
2.60
.

A comparison of the two results indicates that the Friedrich scalar
s_{g}
, associated to a rescaled metric
g=x^{2}\widetilde{g}
where
x
is a boundary defining function verifying (
3.4
), vanishes on the conformal boundary in dimension 4. We propose to prove this result more simply and in fact for all dimensions using the framework of
Section

3
. Let
(\mathcal{M},\mathfrak{I},\widetilde{g})
be an aAdS space of dimension
n+1
. The transformation law of the scalar curvature under conformal rescalings implies that for any boundary defining function
x
of
\mathfrak{I}
, one has

R-\frac{\widetilde{R}}{x^{2}}=n\left(-2\frac{\Box_{g}x}{x}+(n+1)\frac{g^{-1}(dx,dx)}{x^{2}}\right)\,,

(4.24)

where
g:=x^{2}\widetilde{g}
is the associated rescaled metric,
R
is its scalar curvature and
\widetilde{R}
is the scalar curvature of
\widetilde{g}
. Define the Friedrich scalar of
g
by

s_{g}:=\frac{1}{n+1}\left(\Box_{g}x+Lx\right)\,,

where
L=R/2n
is the trace of the Schouten tensor of the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
of
g
. From (
4.24
), one deduces that

s_{g}=\frac{1}{2x}\left(g^{-1}(dx,dx)+\frac{\widetilde{R}}{n(n+1)}\right)\,.

In particular, if
(\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathfrak{I},\widetilde{g})
is solution to the (VE) for the normalised negative cosmological constant
\Lambda=-n(n-1)/2
then

s_{g}=\frac{g^{-1}(dx,dx)-1}{2x}\,.

Hence,
s_{g}
vanishes on the conformal boundary if and only if
g^{-1}(dx,dx)=1+\mathcal{O}\left({x^{2}}\right)
, which is a weaker condition than (
3.4
).

Finally, let us state the equivalent statement of
4.22
for asymptotically hyperbolic spaces. Contrary to the aAdS case, this is not a direct application of
4.21
. Its proof actually relies on a new theorem which will be stated later in the article, see
4.51
.

Proposition 4.23
.

i)

Let
(\mathcal{S},\not{\mathfrak{I}},\widetilde{h})
be a 3-dimensional aH space, in the sense of
3.2
, with smooth rescaled metrics and let
\widetilde{K}_{ij}
be a smooth symmetric 2-tensor field on
\mathcal{S}\setminus\not{\mathfrak{I}}
. If
(\mathcal{S}\setminus\not{\mathfrak{I}},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{K})
is solution to the (
4.21
) for
\Lambda=-3
and if, for any boundary defining function
\Psi
of
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
,

a)

the field
P_{ij}:=\Psi\widetilde{K}_{ij}
extends smoothly to
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
and verifies

\left(D_{\perp}P\right)^{\dagger}_{AB}=s_{h}P_{AB}^{\dagger}\quad\text{on }\not{\mathfrak{I}}\,,

(
4.98
)

b)

all connected components
\mathfrak{S}
of
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
are umbilical in
(\mathcal{S},[h])
with

\not{\mathfrak{K}}_{AB}=s_{h}\;\not{\mathfrak{h}}_{AB}\,,

(
4.99
)

where

\bullet

h:=\Psi^{2}\widetilde{h}
is the rescaled metric associated to
\Psi
,
D
is the Levi-Civita connection of
h
and
s_{h}
is the non-extended 3-dimensional Friedrich scalar field of
h
defined by (
4.11
),

\bullet

\not{\mathfrak{h}}
is the metric induced by
h
on
\mathfrak{S}
,
\not{\mathfrak{K}}
is the extrinsic curvature of
D
on
\mathfrak{S}
,

\bullet

\perp
denotes the outward-pointing normal of
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
with respect to
h
while
A,B
are tangential indices,

\bullet

X^{\dagger}_{AB}
is the trace-free part of the restriction of a 2-tensor
X_{ij}
on
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
,

then for any boundary defining function
\Psi
of
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
, any smooth covector field
\kappa
on
\mathcal{S}
and any smooth scalar field
z
on
\mathcal{S}
which vanishes on
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
,

✦

the fields
K_{ij}
,
Q_{ij}
,
E_{ij}
,
T_{i}
,
M_{ijk}
defined by (
4.22
) extend smoothly to the conformal boundary
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
,

✦

the 10-tuple
(\mathcal{S},h,E,M,\Psi,\kappa,K,z,T,Q)\in\mathscr{D}
is a Riemannian solution of the (
4.9
) for
\Lambda=-3
and verifies the geometric conditions

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{on }\not{\mathscr{I}}\,,

(
4.86a
)

\displaystyle K_{\perp i}+\varepsilon\frac{z}{\Psi}h_{\perp i}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{on }\not{\mathscr{I}}\,,

(
4.86b
)

Moreover, the conformal boundary
\not{\mathscr{I}}
of the 10-tuple coincide with the conformal boundary
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
of the aH space, hence the common name.

ii)

Conversely, let
(\mathcal{S},h,E,M,\Psi,\kappa,K,z,T,Q)\in\mathscr{D}
be a 10-tuple with non-empty conformal boundary
\not{\mathscr{I}}
. If it is a Riemannian solution to the (
4.9
) for
\Lambda=-3
and if it satisfies the geometric conditions (
4.86
) then for any closure
\mathcal{U}
of a connected component of
\mathcal{S}\setminus\not{\mathfrak{I}}
, the triple
(\mathcal{U},\not{\mathfrak{I}}:=\not{\mathscr{I}}\cap\mathcal{U},\widetilde{h}:=|\Psi|^{-2}h)
is a 3-dimensional aH space with smooth rescaled metrics,
(\mathcal{U}\setminus\not{\mathfrak{I}},\widetilde{h}:=|\Psi|^{-2}h,\widetilde{K}:=|\Psi|^{-1}(K-z\Psi^{-1}h))
is solution to the (
4.21
) for
\Lambda=-3
and, for any boundary defining function
\Psi
of
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
, the conditions
a)
and
b)
stated in
i)
hold.

Proof.

i)

By
i)
of
4.21
, the 10-tuple
(\mathcal{S}\setminus\not{\mathfrak{I}},h,E,M,\Psi,\kappa,K,z,T,Q)
is solution to the (CVC) for
\Lambda=-3
. By
4.51
, the hypotheses imply that the unphysical fields extend smoothly to
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
and verifies the geometric conditions (
4.86
). Hence the 10-tuple
(\mathcal{S},h,E,M,\Psi,\kappa,K,z,T,Q)
is in
\mathscr{D}
and, by a continuity argument, is solution to the (CVC) with
\Lambda=-3
. Since
\Psi
is a boundary defining function of
\not{\mathfrak{I}}
, one has
\not{\mathscr{I}}=\not{\mathfrak{I}}
.

ii)

By
ii)
of
4.21
,
(\mathcal{U}\setminus\not{\mathscr{I}},\widetilde{h},\widetilde{K})
is solution to the (VC) with
\Lambda=-3
. By definition of
\mathcal{U}
,
|\Psi|
is positive on
\mathcal{U}
. Thanks to (
4.86a
), one deduces from
Equation

4.19
that
|\Psi|
is a boundary defining function of
\not{\mathscr{I}}\cap\mathcal{U}
and that
(\mathcal{U},\not{\mathscr{I}}\cap\mathcal{U},|\Psi|^{-2}h)
is aH. Then, by
4.51
, the conditions
a)
and
b)
hold. ∎

4.2
Evolution system and propagation of the constraints

This section contains the analytic part of the proof and follows, for the most part, the work of Friedrich
[
38
]
. We first introduce convenient quantities encoding the (CVE), called
zero quantities
, in
Section

4.2.1
. In a specific gauge adapted to the conformal boundary and constructed in
Section

4.2.2
, a suitable evolution system can be derived from some of the zero quantities as shown in
Section

4.2.3
. Particular care has to be taken concerning this step so that the constraints, constituted by the remaining zero quantities, propagate tangentially to the boundary at a later step. If not, the propagation of the constraints cannot be ensured as it would require additional first-order boundary conditions. Friedrich’s method consists in modifying the evolution system by adding some multiple of the constraints. Another strategy is followed in this paper: reducing the number of unknowns by using algebraic symmetries only and choosing judiciously the evolution equations. This was inspired by the work
[
50
]
studying linear fields on AdS which can be seen as a mix of the two methods. The evolution system is then solved in
Section

4.2.4
under certain analytic boundary conditions, see (
4.50
). Finally, the propagation of the constraints is proven in
Section

4.2.5
.

For the sake of simplicity, from now on we will take the normalised negative cosmological constant, that is

\Lambda=-3\,.

4.2.1
Zero quantities

Let us rewrite the (
4.1
) with the so-called
zero quantities
. These vanish identically if and only if the (CVE) hold and are particularly convenient to write the propagation of the constraints, see
Section

4.2.5
.

Definition 4.24
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
be a quintuple,
\acute{\nabla}
be a connection on
\mathcal{M}
,
U_{\alpha\beta}
be a smooth 2-tensor field on
\mathcal{M}
,
\zeta_{\alpha}
be a smooth covector field on
\mathcal{M}
,
s
be a smooth scalar function on
\mathcal{M}
and
(e_{\bf a})
be a smooth frame field on
\mathcal{M}
. Define the corresponding
zero quantities
as follows

\displaystyle\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle:=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}V^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}-\kappa_{\bf a}V^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}\,,

(4.26a)

\displaystyle\varpi^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}

\displaystyle:=\acute{R}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}-\Theta V^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}-2S_{\bf b[c}{}^{\bf ae}U_{\bf d]e}\,,

(4.26b)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\bf ab}

\displaystyle:=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}\kappa_{\bf b}-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}\kappa_{\bf a}+2U_{\bf[ab]}\,,

(4.26c)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\bf abc}

\displaystyle:=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}U_{\bf bc}-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}U_{\bf ac}-\zeta_{\bf d}V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}\,,

(4.26d)

\displaystyle\varkappa_{\bf ab}

\displaystyle:=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}\zeta_{\bf b}+\kappa_{\bf a}\zeta_{\bf b}+\Theta U_{\bf ab}-sg_{\bf ab}\,,

(4.26e)

\displaystyle\mho_{\bf a}

\displaystyle:=e_{\bf a}(s)-\kappa_{\bf a}s+U_{\bf ab}g^{\bf bc}\zeta_{\bf c}\,,

(4.26f)

\displaystyle\varsigma_{\bf a}

\displaystyle:=\zeta_{\bf a}-\Theta\,\kappa_{\bf a}-(d\Theta)_{\bf a}\,,

(4.26g)

\displaystyle\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}

\displaystyle:=\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}-\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf b}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf a}-\langle\omega^{\bf c},[e_{\bf a},e_{\bf b}]\rangle\,,

(4.26h)

\displaystyle\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abc}

\displaystyle:=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}g_{\bf bc}+2\kappa_{\bf a}g_{\bf bc}\,,

(4.26i)

\displaystyle\aleph

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\left(g^{\bf ab}\zeta_{\bf a}\zeta_{\bf b}-1\right)-s\Theta\,,

(4.26j)

where
(\omega^{\bf a})
is the dual coframe field of
(e_{\bf a})
,
\acute{R}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}
are the frame components of the Riemann tensor of
\acute{\nabla}
and

\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}:=\langle\omega^{\bf c},\acute{\nabla}_{e_{\bf a}}e_{\bf b}\rangle\,.

Remarks
.

•

The (CVE) are linked to the zero quantities in the following way

(
4.26a
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:CVE_V}\,,

(
4.26b
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:CVE_Gamma}\,,

(
4.26d
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:CVE_L}\,,

(
4.26e
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:CVE_zeta}\,,

(
4.26g
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:def_zeta}\,,

(
4.26h
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq_torsion_free}\,,

(
4.26i
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{weyl_conn}\,,

(
4.26j
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:CVE_const_bis}\,.

Furthermore, the remaining zero quantities come from the two equations indicated below, which are redundant to the (CVE) but must be added to form a closed system,

\eqref{eq:ZQ_Pi}\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:antisym_part_schouten}\,,\qquad\eqref{eq:ZQ_mho}\leftrightarrow\eqref{eq:grad_s}\,.

•

A general connection
\acute{\nabla}
is introduced because in the propagation of the constraints we do not yet know that it is a Weyl connection. This is indeed encoded by the vanishing of
\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}
and
\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abc}
.

•

By definition of the Riemann tensor, one has

\displaystyle\acute{R}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}

\displaystyle=\langle\omega^{\bf a},\acute{\nabla}_{\bf c}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}e_{\bf b}-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf c}e_{\bf b}-\acute{\nabla}_{[e_{\bf c},e_{\bf d}]}e_{\bf b}\rangle

\displaystyle=e_{\bf c}(\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf d}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf b})-e_{\bf d}(\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf c}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf b})+\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf d}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf c}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf e}-\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf c}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf d}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf e}+\left(\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf d}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf c}-\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf c}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d}\right)\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf e}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf b}+\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf c}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d}\acute{\Gamma}_{\bf e}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf b}\,.\qed

The following lemma holds as a direct consequence of
4.24
.

Lemma 4.25
.

i)

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
be a solution to the (CVE),
\acute{\nabla}
be the Weyl connection
\widehat{\nabla}
associated to
\kappa
with respect to
g
,
U_{\alpha\beta}
be the Schouten tensor
\widehat{L}_{\alpha\beta}
of
\widehat{\nabla}
,
\zeta
be the covector field
\widehat{\zeta}_{g}
defined by (
4.2
),
s
be the extended Friedrich scalar
\widehat{s}_{g}
and
(e_{\bf a})
be a smooth field on
\mathcal{M}
. Then all the zero quantities defined by (
4.26
) vanish identically on
\mathcal{M}
.

ii)

Reciprocally, let
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
,
\acute{\nabla}
be a connection on
\mathcal{M}
,
U_{\alpha\beta}
be a smooth 2-tensor field on
\mathcal{M}
,
\zeta_{\alpha}
be a smooth covector field on
\mathcal{M}
,
s
be a smooth scalar field on
\mathcal{M}
and
(e_{\bf a})
be a smooth frame field on
\mathcal{M}
such that all zero quantities defined by (
4.26
) vanish identically on
\mathcal{M}
. Then
\acute{\nabla}
is the Weyl connection
\widehat{\nabla}
associated to
\kappa
with respect to
g
,
U_{\alpha\beta}
is its Schouten tensor
\widehat{L}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\zeta_{\alpha}
is the covector field
(\widehat{\zeta}_{g})_{\alpha}
,
s
is the extended Friedrich scalar
\widehat{s}_{g}
and
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)
is solution to the (CVE) with
\Lambda=-3
.

Proof.

The proof of
i)
is immediate. For
ii)
, one has to deduce in this order that
\acute{\nabla}
is the Weyl connection
\widehat{\nabla}
associated to
\kappa
with respect to
g
from
\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}=0
and
\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abc}=0
, that
U_{\alpha\beta}
is the Schouten tensor of
\widehat{\nabla}
from
\varpi^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}=0
(uniqueness of the decomposition), that
\zeta_{\alpha}
is the covector field
(\widehat{\zeta}_{g})_{\alpha}
from
\varsigma_{\bf a}=0
, that
s
is the extended Friedrich scalar
\widehat{s}_{g}
from
g^{\bf ab}\varkappa_{\bf ab}=0
. It follows that
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)
is solution to the (CVE).
∎

In order to propagate the constraints, one needs a system of differential equations on the zero quantities. This is given by the next proposition.

Proposition 4.26
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
be a quintuple and
\acute{\nabla}
,
U_{\alpha\beta}
,
\zeta_{\alpha}
,
s
,
(e_{\bf a})
be as in
4.24
. Assume furthermore that
(\mathcal{M},g)
is oriented. Then the zero quantities of
4.24
verify the following differential equations

\displaystyle g^{\bf be}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\varpi^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bae}V^{\bf be}{}_{\bf cd}-V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf b[c}\varpi^{\bf b}{}_{\bf d]ae}

\displaystyle\quad-g^{\bf ep}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle\quad+g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+\kappa_{\bf a}g^{\bf ab}\left(\varphi_{\bf cdb}-\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epb}V^{\bf ep}{}_{\bf cd}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{2}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}\Pi_{\bf ae}+g^{\bf ef}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}\,,

(4.27a)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}

\displaystyle=-\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf f}{}_{\bf b}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|c]f}-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}\varsigma_{\bf c]}+S_{\bf e[a}{}^{\bf df}\varrho_{\bf bc]f}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(g^{\bf df}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab|e|}-g^{\bf dp}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[a|pq|}g_{\bf b|e|}\right)U_{\bf c]f}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\Theta}{6}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}\epsilon{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}{}^{\bf qr}\varphi_{\bf qrf}-\frac{\Theta}{6}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}V^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fst}F_{\bf abce}{}^{\bf stuvfd}\,,

(4.27b)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\Pi_{\bf bc]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|d|}\kappa_{\bf c]}-\frac{1}{2}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf[cab]}\kappa_{\bf d}+\varrho_{\bf[abc]}\,,

(4.27c)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{[\bf a}\varrho_{\bf bc]d}

\displaystyle=\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|e|}U_{\bf c]d}-\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf[cab]}U_{\bf ed}-\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}U_{\bf c]e}-V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\varkappa_{\bf c]e}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{6}\zeta_{\bf e}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}\epsilon{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d}{}^{\bf qr}\varphi_{\bf qrf}-\frac{1}{6}\zeta_{\bf e}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}V^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fst}F_{\bf abcd}{}^{\bf stuvfe}\,,

(4.27d)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\varkappa_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}\zeta_{\bf c}-\frac{1}{2}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}\zeta_{\bf d}-\varsigma_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}+\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf c}\Pi_{\bf ab}

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa_{\bf[a}\varkappa_{\bf b]c}+\frac{\Theta}{2}\varrho_{\bf abc}-s\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab]c}+g_{\bf c[a}\mho_{\bf b]}\,,

(4.27e)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\mho_{\bf b]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}e_{\bf c}(s)-\frac{s}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}+\kappa_{\bf[a}\mho_{\bf b]}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\bf cd}\zeta_{\bf d}\varrho_{\bf abc}

\displaystyle\quad-g^{\bf ce}g^{\bf df}\zeta_{\bf d}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[a|ef|}U_{\bf b]c}+g^{\bf cd}\varkappa_{\bf[a|d|}U_{\bf b]c}\,,

(4.27f)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\varsigma_{\bf b]}

\displaystyle=\varkappa_{\bf[ab]}-\kappa_{\bf[a}\varsigma_{\bf b]}-\frac{\Theta}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}(d\Theta)_{\bf c}\,,

(4.27g)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf b}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf c]}

\displaystyle=-\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf[cab]}-\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf c]}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}\,,

(4.27h)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf b]cd}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ecd}-g_{\bf e(c}\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d)ab}+g_{\bf cd}\Pi_{\bf ab}-2\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf b]cd}\,,

(4.27i)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}\aleph

\displaystyle=g^{\bf bc}\zeta_{\bf c}\varkappa_{\bf ab}+s\varsigma_{\bf a}-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf b}\zeta_{\bf c}g^{\bf bd}g^{\bf ce}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ade}-\Theta\mho_{\bf a}\,,

(4.27j)

where

\displaystyle G^{\bf uvabcd}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}g^{\bf uv}g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf cd}-g^{\bf au}g^{\bf bv}g^{\bf cd}-g^{\bf cu}g^{\bf dv}g^{\bf ab}\,,

(4.28a)

\displaystyle F_{\bf abce}{}^{\bf stuvfd}

\displaystyle:=\epsilon_{\bf pe}{}^{\bf st}\epsilon_{\bf xabc}G^{\bf uvxfdp}+\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}\epsilon{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf exy}G^{\bf uvsxty}\,.

(4.28b)

Remark
.

Note that the right hand sides of equations (
4.27
) are homogeneous terms of the zero quantities except the last term in the right hand side of (
4.27
). This can be addressed by adding
\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf cde}
as a zero quantity and expressing
\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf cde}
with homogeneous terms. However, this will not be necessary in our case thanks to a triangular structure in the propagation of the constraints, see the proof of
4.34
.
∎

Proof.

First, let us derive some useful identities.

•

Equations (
2.39
) and (
2.41
) can be generalised for an arbitrary connection
\acute{\nabla}
as follows

\acute{\nabla}_{\alpha}(Q\star)^{\alpha}{}_{\beta\lambda\xi}-(\star J)_{\lambda\xi\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\lambda\xi}{}^{\mu\nu}\left(\acute{\nabla}_{\alpha}Q^{\alpha}{}_{\beta\mu\nu}-J_{\mu\nu\beta}\right)+\frac{1}{2}Q^{\alpha}{}_{\beta\mu\nu}\acute{\nabla}_{\alpha}\epsilon_{\lambda\xi}{}^{\mu\nu}\,,

\displaystyle 3\left(\acute{\nabla}_{[\alpha}Q^{\rho}{}_{|\nu|\lambda\xi]}-S_{\nu[\alpha}{}^{\rho\gamma}J_{\lambda\xi]\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{\beta}{}_{\alpha\lambda\xi}g^{\rho\mu}\left(\acute{\nabla}_{\sigma}(Q\star)^{\sigma}{}_{\beta\mu\nu}-(\star J)_{\mu\nu\beta}\right)+(Q\star)^{\sigma}{}_{\beta\mu\nu}\acute{\nabla}_{\sigma}\left(\epsilon^{\beta}{}_{\alpha\lambda\xi}g^{\rho\mu}\right)\,.

•

By definition of the volume form
\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}
of
g

\acute{\nabla}_{\sigma}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}=\left(\frac{g^{\xi\rho}}{2}\acute{\vartheta}_{\sigma\xi\rho}-4\kappa_{\sigma}\right)\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}\,,

Thus

\acute{\nabla}_{\sigma}\left(\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}g^{\mu\xi}g^{\nu\rho}\right)=\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf\sigma\chi\psi}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\mu\nu}G^{\chi\psi\mu\xi\nu\rho}\,.

In particular,

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\sigma}\left(\epsilon^{\beta}{}_{\alpha\lambda\xi}g^{\rho\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\sigma}\left(\epsilon_{\nu\alpha\lambda\xi}g^{\bf\nu\beta}g^{\rho\mu}\right)=\acute{\vartheta}_{\sigma\chi\psi}\epsilon_{\nu\alpha\lambda\xi}G^{\bf\chi\psi\nu\beta\rho\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\alpha}\epsilon_{\lambda\xi}{}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\alpha}\left(\epsilon_{\lambda\xi\rho\sigma}g^{\mu\rho}g^{\nu\sigma}\right)=\acute{\vartheta}_{\alpha\chi\psi}\epsilon_{\lambda\xi\rho\sigma}G^{\chi\psi\mu\rho\nu\sigma}\,.

•

By equation (
2.42
), for all 1-form
\omega_{\alpha}

S_{\lambda[\alpha}{}^{\mu\nu}V^{\sigma}{}_{|\nu|\beta\gamma]}\omega_{\sigma}=V^{\mu}{}_{\lambda[\alpha\beta}\omega_{\gamma]}\,.

(4.29)

Combining the three points above, one deduces that

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}\kappa_{\bf c]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}\epsilon{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}{}^{\bf qr}\varphi_{\bf qrf}+\frac{1}{3}(V\star)^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fpe}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}\epsilon_{\bf sabc}G^{\bf uvsfdp}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{6}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}g^{\bf dp}V^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fst}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}\epsilon_{\bf pexy}G^{\bf uvsxty}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}\epsilon{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}{}^{\bf qr}\varphi_{\bf qrf}+\frac{1}{6}\epsilon_{\bf pe}{}^{\bf xy}V^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fxy}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}\epsilon_{\bf sabc}G^{\bf uvsfdp}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{6}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}g^{\bf dp}V^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fst}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}\epsilon_{\bf pexy}G^{\bf uvsxty}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\epsilon^{\bf f}{}_{\bf abc}\epsilon{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}{}^{\bf qr}\varphi_{\bf qrf}+\frac{1}{6}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ruv}V^{\bf r}{}_{\bf fst}F_{\bf abce}{}^{\bf stuvfd}\,.

(4.30)

Now let us prove equations (
4.27
).

•

Equation (
4.27
)

\displaystyle g^{\bf be}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\left(g^{\bf be}\varphi_{\bf cdb}\right)+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}-2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf be}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\left(g^{\bf be}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}V^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bcd}-\kappa_{\bf a}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}\right)+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}-2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf be}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\left(-g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+3\kappa_{\bf a}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}\right)+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}-2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf be}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e]}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}+\left(-g^{\bf ep}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epq}+2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf ef}\right)\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+g^{\bf ef}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}

\displaystyle\quad+g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+3V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf e]}+3\kappa_{\bf a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}V^{\bf ea}{}_{\bf cd}+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle\quad-2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf be}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e]}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}+\left(-g^{\bf ep}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epq}+2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf ef}\right)\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+g^{\bf ef}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}

\displaystyle\quad+g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+3V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{\bf ae}-U_{\bf[ae]}\right)+3\kappa_{\bf a}g^{\bf ab}\left(\varphi_{\bf cdb}-\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epb}V^{\bf ep}{}_{\bf cd}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}-2\kappa_{\bf e}g^{\bf be}\varphi_{\bf cdb}

\displaystyle=-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e]}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-g^{\bf ep}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}+g^{\bf ab}g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf eaf}\varphi_{\bf cdb}+g^{\bf ef}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}

\displaystyle\quad+3V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{\bf ae}-U_{\bf ae}\right)+\kappa_{\bf a}g^{\bf ab}\left(\varphi_{\bf cdb}-\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf epb}V^{\bf ep}{}_{\bf cd}\right)+g^{\bf ef}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abf}\,.

Moreover,

\displaystyle-\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e]}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\frac{1}{2}\acute{R}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bae}V^{\bf be}{}_{\bf cd}-\frac{1}{2}\acute{R}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf bae}V^{\bf ab}{}_{\bf cd}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\acute{R}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf cae}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf bd}+\frac{1}{2}\acute{R}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf dae}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cb}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\acute{R}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bae}V^{\bf be}{}_{\bf cd}-V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf b[c}\acute{R}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf d]ae}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\left(\varpi^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bae}+2S_{\bf b[a}{}^{\bf af}U_{\bf e]f}\right)V^{\bf be}{}_{\bf cd}

\displaystyle\quad-V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf b[c}\left(\varpi^{\bf b}{}_{\bf d]ae}+2S_{\bf d][a}{}^{\bf bf}U_{\bf e]f}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\left(\varpi^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bae}+S_{\bf ba}{}^{\bf af}U_{\bf ef}\right)V^{\bf be}{}_{\bf cd}

\displaystyle\quad-V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf b[c}\left(\varpi^{\bf b}{}_{\bf d]ae}+2S_{\bf d]a}{}^{\bf bf}U_{\bf ef}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}-\varpi^{\bf a}{}_{\bf bae}V^{\bf be}{}_{\bf cd}-V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf b[c}\varpi^{\bf b}{}_{\bf d]ae}+3V^{\bf ae}{}_{\bf cd}U_{\bf ae}\,.

Hence the result.

•

Equation (
4.27
). Note that by definition (
2.7
) of the tensor
S_{\alpha\beta}{}^{\mu\nu}
,

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}S_{\bf bc}{}^{\bf de}

\displaystyle=-g^{\bf de}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}g_{\bf bc}-g_{\bf bc}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}g^{\bf de}

\displaystyle=-g^{\bf de}\left(\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abc}-2\kappa_{\bf a}g_{\bf bc}\right)-g_{\bf bc}\left(-g^{\bf dp}g^{\bf eq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf apq}+2\kappa_{\bf a}g^{\bf de}\right)

\displaystyle=-g^{\bf de}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf abc}+g_{\bf bc}g^{\bf dp}g^{\bf eq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf apq}\,.

Now, with (
4.29
),

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-\Theta\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}(d\Theta)_{\bf c]}

\displaystyle\quad+2S_{\bf e[a}{}^{\bf df}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}U_{\bf c]f}-2\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\left(S_{\bf|e|b}{}^{\bf df}\right)U_{\bf c]f}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-\Theta\left(\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}\kappa_{\bf c]}\right)-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}\left(\varsigma_{\bf c]}-\zeta_{\bf c]}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{\bf e[a}{}^{\bf df}\left(\varrho_{\bf bc]f}-V^{\bf p}{}_{\bf|f|bc]}\zeta_{\bf p}\right)+2\left(g^{\bf df}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab|e|}-g^{\bf dp}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[a|pq|}g_{\bf b|e|}\right)U_{\bf c]f}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-\Theta\left(\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}\kappa_{\bf c]}\right)-V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e[ab}\varsigma_{\bf c]}

\displaystyle\quad+S_{\bf e[a}{}^{\bf df}\varrho_{\bf bc]f}+2\left(g^{\bf df}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab|e|}-g^{\bf dp}g^{\bf fq}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[a|pq|}g_{\bf b|e|}\right)U_{\bf c]f}\,.

The second Bianchi identity for
\acute{\nabla}
gives

\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|bc]}=-\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf f}{}_{\bf b}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf|e|c]f}\,,

and one concludes with (
4.30
).

•

Equation (
4.27c
)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\Pi_{\bf bc]}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b}\kappa_{\bf c]}+2\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}U_{\bf bc]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|d|}\kappa_{\bf c]}-\frac{1}{2}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf[cab]}\kappa_{\bf d}+2\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}U_{\bf bc]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|d|}\kappa_{\bf c]}-\frac{1}{2}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf[cab]}\kappa_{\bf d}+\varrho_{\bf[abc]}\,.

•

Equation (
4.27
)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{[\bf a}\varrho_{\bf bc]d}

\displaystyle=2\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\vphantom{[}\bf b}U_{\bf c]d}-\zeta_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf|d|bc]}-V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf c]}\zeta_{\bf e}

\displaystyle=\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|e|}U_{\bf c]d}-\acute{R}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf[cab]}U_{\bf ed}-\acute{R}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}U_{\bf c]e}

\displaystyle\quad-\zeta_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf|d|bc]}-V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf c]}\zeta_{\bf e}

\displaystyle=\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|e|}U_{\bf c]d}-\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf[cab]}U_{\bf ed}-\left(\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}+\Theta V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\right)U_{\bf c]e}

\displaystyle\quad-\zeta_{\bf e}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf|d|bc]}-V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\left(\varkappa_{\bf c]e}-\kappa_{\bf c]}\zeta_{\bf e}-\Theta U_{\bf c]e}\right)

\displaystyle=\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf|e|}U_{\bf c]d}-\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf[cab]}U_{\bf ed}-\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}U_{\bf c]e}-V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\varkappa_{\bf c]e}

\displaystyle\quad-\zeta_{\bf e}\left(\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf|d|bc]}-V^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d[ab}\kappa_{\bf c]}\right)\,,

and one concludes using (
4.30
).

Equation (
4.27
)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\varkappa_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}\zeta_{\bf c}+\zeta_{\bf c}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}-\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}\zeta_{\bf c}+(d\Theta)_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle\quad+\Theta\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}-s\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}g_{\bf b]c}+g_{\bf c[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}\zeta_{\bf c}-\frac{1}{2}\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}\zeta_{\bf d}+\zeta_{\bf c}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}-\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}\zeta_{\bf c}

\displaystyle\quad+(d\Theta)_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}+\Theta\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}-s\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}g_{\bf b]c}+g_{\bf c[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}\zeta_{\bf c}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}+\Theta V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}+2S_{\bf c[a}{}^{\bf de}U_{\bf b]e}\right)\zeta_{\bf d}

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta_{\bf c}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}-U_{\bf[ab]}\right)-\kappa_{\bf[a}\left(\varkappa_{\bf b]c}-\Theta U_{\bf b]c}+sg_{\bf b]c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(d\Theta)_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}+\frac{\Theta}{2}\left(\varrho_{\bf abc}+\zeta_{\bf d}V^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-s\left(\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab]c}-2\kappa_{\bf[a}g_{\bf b]c}\right)+g_{\bf c[a}\left(\mho_{\bf b]}+\kappa_{\bf b]}s-U_{\bf b]d}g^{\bf de}\zeta_{\bf e}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}\zeta_{\bf c}-\frac{1}{2}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}\zeta_{\bf d}-\zeta_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}+\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf c}\Pi_{\bf ab}

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa_{\bf[a}\left(\varkappa_{\bf b]c}-\Theta U_{\bf b]c}\right)+(d\Theta)_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}+\frac{\Theta}{2}\varrho_{\bf abc}-s\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab]c}+g_{\bf c[a}\mho_{\bf b]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf d}\zeta_{\bf c}-\frac{1}{2}\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf cab}\zeta_{\bf d}-\varsigma_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}+\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf c}\Pi_{\bf ab}

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa_{\bf[a}\varkappa_{\bf b]c}+\frac{\Theta}{2}\varrho_{\bf abc}-s\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[ab]c}+g_{\bf c[a}\mho_{\bf b]}\,.

•

Equation (

)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\mho_{\bf b]}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}s-s\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}+\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}s+g^{\bf cd}\zeta_{\bf d}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}U_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle\quad-g^{\bf ce}g^{\bf df}\zeta_{\bf d}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}g_{\bf|ef|}U_{\bf b]c}+g^{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\zeta_{\bf|d|}U_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}e_{\bf c}(s)-s\left(\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}-U_{\bf[ab]}\right)+\kappa_{\bf[a}\left(\mho_{\bf b]}-U_{\bf b]c}g^{\bf cd}\zeta_{\bf d}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}g^{\bf cd}\zeta_{\bf d}\varrho_{\bf abc}-g^{\bf ce}g^{\bf df}\zeta_{\bf d}\left(\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[a|ef|}-2g_{\bf ef}\kappa_{\bf[a}\right)U_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle\quad+g^{\bf cd}\left(\varkappa_{\bf[a|d|}-\zeta_{\bf d}\kappa_{\bf[a}+sg_{\bf d[a}\right)U_{\bf b]c}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}e_{\bf c}(s)-\frac{s}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}+\kappa_{\bf[a}\mho_{\bf b]}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\bf cd}\zeta_{\bf d}\varrho_{\bf abc}

\displaystyle\quad-g^{\bf ce}g^{\bf df}\zeta_{\bf d}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf[a|ef|}U_{\bf b]c}+g^{\bf cd}\varkappa_{\bf[a|d|}U_{\bf b]c}\,.

Equation (
4.27g
)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\varsigma_{\bf b]}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\zeta_{\bf b]}-(d\Theta)_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}-\Theta\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}-\acute{\nabla}_{[\bf a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}\Theta

\displaystyle=\left(\varkappa_{\bf[ab]}-\kappa_{\bf[a}\zeta_{\bf b]}-\Theta U_{\bf[ab]}\right)+\kappa_{\bf[a}(d\Theta)_{\bf b]}-\Theta\left(\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}-U_{\bf[ab]}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}(d\Theta)_{\bf c}

\displaystyle=\varkappa_{\bf[ab]}-\kappa_{\bf[a}\varsigma_{\bf b]}-\frac{\Theta}{2}\Pi_{\bf ab}-\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf c}{}_{\bf b}(d\Theta)_{\bf c}\,.

•

Equation (
4.27h
). With the first Bianchi identity for
\acute{\nabla}
,

\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf b}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf c]}=-\acute{R}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf[cab]}-\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf c]}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}=-\varpi^{\bf d}{}_{\bf[cab]}-\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf[a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf c]}{}^{\bf d}{}_{\bf e}\,.

Equation (
4.27i
)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf b]cd}

\displaystyle=\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}g_{\bf cd}+2g_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}-2\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf b]}g_{\bf cd}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf e}g_{\bf cd}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\acute{R}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf cab}g_{\bf ed}+\acute{R}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf dab}g_{\bf ce}\right)+2g_{\bf cd}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf[a}\kappa_{\bf b]}-2\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf b]cd}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ecd}-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf cab}g_{\bf ed}+2S_{\bf c[a}{}^{\bf ef}U_{\bf b]f}g_{\bf ed}+\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf dab}g_{\bf ce}

\displaystyle\quad+2S_{\bf d[a}{}^{\bf ef}U_{\bf b]f}g_{\bf ce}\Big)+g_{\bf cd}\left(\Pi_{\bf ab}-2U_{\bf[ab]}\right)-2\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf b]cd}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\acute{\Sigma}_{\bf a}{}^{\bf e}{}_{\bf b}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ecd}-g_{\bf e(c}\varpi^{\bf e}{}_{\bf d)ab}+g_{\bf cd}\Pi_{\bf ab}-2\kappa_{\bf[a}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf b]cd}\,.

•

Equation (
4.27j
)

\displaystyle\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}\aleph

\displaystyle=g^{\bf bc}\zeta_{\bf c}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}\zeta_{\bf b}-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf b}\zeta_{\bf c}g^{\bf bd}g^{\bf ce}\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}g_{\bf de}-\Theta\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}s-s\acute{\nabla}_{\bf a}\Theta

\displaystyle=g^{\bf bc}\zeta_{\bf c}\left(\varkappa_{\bf ab}-\kappa_{\bf a}\zeta_{\bf b}-\Theta U_{\bf ab}+sg_{\bf ab}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf b}\zeta_{\bf c}g^{\bf bd}g^{\bf ce}\left(\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ade}-2\kappa_{\bf a}g_{\bf de}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\Theta\left(\mho_{\bf a}+\kappa_{\bf a}s-U_{\bf ab}g^{\bf bc}\zeta_{\bf c}\right)-s(d\Theta)_{\bf a}

\displaystyle=g^{\bf bc}\zeta_{\bf c}\varkappa_{\bf ab}+s\varsigma_{\bf a}-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{\bf b}\zeta_{\bf c}g^{\bf bd}g^{\bf ce}\acute{\vartheta}_{\bf ade}-\Theta\mho_{\bf a}\,.\qed

4.2.2
Gauge construction

In order to solve the (CVE), one needs to fix the gauge invariances presented in
4.4
to obtain a suitable hyperbolic system. A gauge choice for the (CVE) consists of choosing a conformal factor
\Theta
, a Weyl connection
\widehat{\nabla}
, an orthonormal frame field
(e_{\bf a})
and a coordinate system
(y^{\mu})
. The next proposition states the existence of a gauge choice which is adapted to the conformal boundary.

Proposition 4.27

(Adaptation of Friedrich
[
38
, Section 5.1]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)\in\mathscr{E}
be a time-oriented and oriented solution to the (
4.1
) for
\Lambda=-3
. Assume that there exists a compact spacelike hypersurface
\mathcal{S}_{\star}
with (non-empty) boundary
\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}
such that

\displaystyle\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{\star}\cap\mathscr{I}\,,

(4.31a)

\displaystyle n_{\star}(\Theta)

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{on }\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}\,,

(4.31b)

where
n_{\star}
is the future-directed unit normal vector field of
\mathcal{S}_{\star}
with respect to
g
. The second assumption means that the normal
n_{\star}
is tangent to
\mathscr{I}
on
\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}
.

Then for all
p\in\mathcal{S}_{\star}
there exists a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
of
p
in
\mathcal{M}
and a gauge choice
\left(\breve{\Theta},\widecheck{\nabla},(e_{\bf a}),(y^{\mu})\right)
on
\mathcal{U}
– giving rise to a solution
\left(\mathcal{U},\breve{g},\breve{V},\breve{\Theta},\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\right)
of the (CVE) which is gauge-equivalent to
\left(\mathcal{U},g,V,\Theta,\kappa\right)
– verifying the following core properties

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\star}:=\mathcal{S_{\star}}\cap\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle=\{q\in\mathcal{U}\mid y^{0}(q)=0\}\,,

(4.32a)

\displaystyle\breve{\Theta}_{\star}:=\breve{\Theta}|_{\mathcal{U}_{\star}}

\displaystyle\geq 0\,,

(4.32b)

\displaystyle e_{\bf i}{}^{0}|_{\mathcal{U}_{\star}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.32c)

\displaystyle y^{3}

\displaystyle\geq 0\,,

(4.32d)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}{}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\bf 0}{}^{\mu}\,,

(4.32e)

\displaystyle\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf 0}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf a}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.32f)

\displaystyle\widecheck{L}_{\bf 0a}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.32g)

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.32h)

\displaystyle\breve{g}_{\bf ab}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\bf ab}\,,

(4.32i)

\displaystyle\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf 0123}

\displaystyle=+1\,,

(4.32j)

and the following additional properties

\displaystyle\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}}(y^{0},\dots,y^{3})

\displaystyle=(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}(y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})\,,

(4.33a)

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}(y^{0},\dots,y^{3})

\displaystyle=-(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}(y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})\,y^{0}\,,

(4.33b)

\displaystyle\breve{\Theta}(y^{0},\dots,y^{3})

\displaystyle=\breve{\Theta}_{\star}(y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})\left(1-f_{\star}(y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})\frac{(y^{0})^{2}}{2}\right)\,,

(4.33c)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\cap\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle=\{q\in\mathcal{U}\mid y^{3}(q)=0\}\,,

(4.33d)

where
(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}:=\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}}|_{\mathcal{U}_{\star}}
and
f_{\star}:=\breve{\Theta}_{\star}^{-1}(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}
are smooth functions on
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
.

Remarks
.

•

Only the additional properties rely on the hypothesis that
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)
is a solution to the (CVE).

•

The proof has been improved from Friedrich’s original version which is based on
4.18
,
ii)
of
4.20
and
2.45
. The modifications enable to treat all conformal geodesics at once and only refers to the (CVE), instead of discriminating the conformal geodesics by whether they start on the conformal boundary or not and referring to the (VE).

•

As pointed out by Friedrich, one has
e_{\bf i}{}^{0}=0
on
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
but not necessarily for all times
y^{0}
. Thus the vector fields
(e_{\bf i})
are in general not tangent to the spacelike hypersurfaces of constant time coordinate
y^{0}
. They only provide a foliation of
\mathcal{U}
by 3-dimensional spacelike distributions. ∎

Proof.

Apart from the modifications mentioned in the above remarks, the proof follows
[
38
, Section 5.1]
. We divide the proof into five steps.

•

Step 1
: Modifying the quintuple
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)
via the gauge invariances

Taking advantage of the gauge invariance of the (CVE) described in
4.4
, one can assume that

\Theta\geq 0\quad\text{on }\mathcal{S}_{\star}\,,\qquad\langle\widehat{\zeta}_{g},n_{\star}\rangle=0\quad\text{on }\mathcal{S}_{\star}\,,\qquad\widehat{s}_{g}=0\quad\text{on }\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}\,.

(4.34)

Indeed,

✦

By hypothesis (
4.31a
), the function
\Theta
does not change sign on
\mathcal{S}_{\star}
. Thus, one can use the invariance by sign change
\Signchg
to ensure that the gauge-equivalent quintuple thereby obtained satisfies the inequality in (
4.34
).

✦

One can find a smooth covector field
\omega
on
\mathcal{M}
such that

\displaystyle g^{-1}(\omega,d\Theta)

\displaystyle=-\widehat{s}_{g}\quad

\displaystyle\text{on }\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}\,,

\displaystyle\langle\omega,n_{\star}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\Theta^{-1}\langle\widehat{\zeta}_{g},n_{\star}\rangle\quad

\displaystyle\text{on }\mathcal{S}_{\star}\,.

Note that the right hand side of the second equation is smooth up to
\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}
thanks to hypotheses (
4.31a
)-(
4.31b
). Applying the Weyl connection change
\Weylchg_{\omega}
guarantees that the gauge-equivalent quintuple thereby obtained satisfies the last two equations of (
4.34
) thanks to (
4.3
) and (
4.5
).

The two operations described above can be performed in any order and do not conflict by (
4.3
) and
4.7
.

•

Step 2
: Choice of conformal geodesics and construction of
\mathcal{U}

Let us assume that (
4.34
) hold thanks to step 1. Let
\widehat{\nabla}
be the Weyl connection associated to
\kappa
with respect to
g
. From each point
q
of
\mathcal{S}_{\star}
, start a future-directed
\widehat{\nabla}
-conformal geodesic
(x,\widehat{\beta})
with initial parameter
\tau_{\star}=0
and initial data

x_{\star}=q\,,\quad\dot{x}_{\star}=n_{\star}|_{q}\,,\quad\widehat{\beta}_{\star}=0\,.

(4.35)

Fix
p\in\mathcal{S}_{\star}
and let
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
be an open neighbourhood of
p
in
\mathcal{S}_{\star}
. Take
\overline{\tau}>0
such that all the conformal geodesics starting on
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
are defined on
(-\overline{\tau},+\overline{\tau})
and do not intersect (that is to say no caustic forms). Define
\mathcal{U}
as the union of the images of the interval
(-\overline{\tau},+\overline{\tau})
under the curves
x
of the conformal geodesics described above. Then
\mathcal{U}
is an open neighbourhood of
p
in
\mathcal{M}
such that

i)

it is covered by the conformal geodesics
(x,\widehat{\beta})
defined above,

ii)

these conformal geodesics do not intersect on
\mathcal{U}
,

iii)

the intersection of
\mathcal{U}
with each conformal geodesic
(x,\widehat{\beta})
is connected.

As a consequence, one has a smooth congruence of timelike conformal geodesics on
\mathcal{U}
.

•

Step 3
: Constructing
\widecheck{\nabla}
,
\breve{g}
,
(y^{\mu})
and
(e_{\bf a})

Let
\widecheck{\nabla}
and
\breve{g}
be respectively the Weyl connection on
\mathcal{U}
and the metric on
\mathcal{U}
conformally related to
g|_{\mathcal{U}}
given by
2.44
. Modifying accordingly the quintuple is done by a confomorphism of conformal factor
\Psi=\breve{\Theta}/\Theta
and a Weyl connection change of covector field
\omega=\widehat{\beta}
. Note that
\widecheck{\nabla}
and
\breve{g}
coincide respectively with
\widehat{\nabla}
and
g
on
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
due to the particular choice of initial data (
4.35
).

Up to reducing
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
, one can take

✦

(y^{i})=(y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})
a coordinate system on
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
such that

\displaystyle y^{3}

\displaystyle\geq 0\,,

(4.36a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\star}\cap\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\{q\in\mathcal{U}_{\star}\mid y^{3}(q)=0\}\,,

(4.36b)

✦

(e_{\bf i})=(e_{\bf 1},e_{\bf 2},e_{\bf 3})
a frame field on
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
such that

g(e_{\bf i},e_{\bf j})=\delta_{\bf i,j}\,.

(4.37)

Construct now

✦

a conformal Gaussian coordinate system
(y^{\mu})
on
\mathcal{U}
by

y^{0}(x(\tau)):=\tau\,,\qquad y^{i}(x(\tau)):=y^{i}(x(0))\,,

✦

a frame field
(e_{\bf a})
on
\mathcal{U}
by

e_{\bf 0}:=\dot{x}\,,\qquad\widecheck{\nabla}_{\dot{x}}e_{\bf i}=0\,.

(4.38)

•

Step 4
: Verifying the core properties (
4.32
)

One derives directly (
4.32a
), (
4.32c
), (
4.32d
) and (
4.32e
) while (
4.32b
) comes from (
4.34
) ensured in step 1. Moreover,
2.44
and (
4.38
) give

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{e_{\bf 0}}e_{\bf a}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\widecheck{L}(e_{\bf 0},.)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{g}(e_{\bf 0},e_{\bf 0})

\displaystyle=-1\,,

or in frame components

\displaystyle\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf 0}{}^{\bf b}{}_{\bf a}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\widecheck{L}_{\bf 0a}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\breve{g}_{\bf 00}

\displaystyle=-1\,.

Hence (
4.32f
) and (
4.32g
). Additionally,

e_{\bf 0}(\breve{g}_{\bf ab})=\widecheck{\nabla}_{e_{\bf 0}}\breve{g}_{\bf ab}=-2(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}\breve{g}_{\bf ab}\,,

where
\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}
is the covector field associated to
\widecheck{\nabla}
with respect to
\breve{g}
. Since
\breve{g}_{\bf 00}=-1
, one deduces that
(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}=0
and that the functions
\breve{g}_{\bf ab}
are constant along the conformal geodesics. By (
4.35
) and (
4.37
), it follows that
(e_{\bf a})
is a
\breve{g}
-orthonormal frame field, that is (
4.32i
). Finally, up to switching
e_{\bf 1}
and
e_{\bf 2}
, one can assume that

\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf 0123}=+1\,.

•

Step 5
: Verifying the additional properties (
4.33
)

Assume that
(\mathcal{M},g,V,\Theta,\kappa)
is solution to the (CVE). Then so it is of the quintuple
\left(\mathcal{U},\breve{g},\breve{V},\breve{\Theta},\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\right)
because it is gauge-equivalent to
\left(\mathcal{U},g,V,\Theta,\kappa\right)
.

By the transformation laws (
4.3
),

\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}}=\widehat{\zeta}_{\breve{g}}+\breve{\Theta}\widehat{\beta}=\frac{\breve{\Theta}}{\Theta}\widehat{\zeta}_{g}+\breve{\Theta}\widehat{\beta}\,.

In particular, since
\breve{\Theta}_{\star}=\Theta_{\star}:=\Theta|_{\mathcal{U}_{\star}}
and
\widehat{\beta}_{\star}=0
, one has
(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}=(\widehat{\zeta}_{g})_{\star}
. It follows from (
4.6b
), derived from the (CVE), that
(\widehat{s}_{g})_{\star}=(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}
.

By
4.25

i)
, all zero quantities vanish on
\mathcal{U}
. Using the core properties (
4.32
), one deduces that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mho_{\bf 0}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})\,,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\varkappa_{\bf 00}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf 0}((\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0})+\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}}\,,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\varsigma_{\bf 0}

\displaystyle=(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}-(d\breve{\Theta})_{\bf 0}\,.

Yet with (
4.34
) ensured in step 1, one has

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}=(\widehat{\zeta}_{g})_{\bf 0}=\langle\widehat{\zeta}_{g},n_{\star}\rangle=0\quad\text{on }\mathcal{U}_{\star}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\star}:=\breve{\Theta}_{\star}^{-1}(\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}})_{\star}=\Theta_{\star}^{-1}(\widehat{s}_{g})_{\star}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathcal{U}_{\star},\mathbb{R})\,.

Hence (
4.33a
)-(
4.33c
).

Finally, let us show (
4.33d
). If
\sup_{\overline{\mathcal{U}_{\star}}}f_{\star}>0
, let us reduce
\overline{\tau}
such that

\overline{\tau}<\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sup_{\overline{\mathcal{U}_{\star}}}f_{\star}}}\,,

so that the inequality

1-f_{\star}(y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})\frac{(y^{0})^{2}}{2}>0

(4.39)

is enforced everywhere on
\mathcal{U}
. For
q\in\mathcal{U}
, one has

\displaystyle y^{3}(q)=0

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\exists\,(x,\widehat{\beta})\text{ in the congruence and}

[by Gaussian coord.]

\displaystyle\tau\in(-\overline{\tau},+\overline{\tau})\text{ such that}

\displaystyle q=x(\tau)\text{ and }y^{3}(x_{\star})=0

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\exists\,(x,\widehat{\beta})\text{ in the congruence and}

\displaystyle[\text{by }\eqref{gauge_choice_coord}]

\displaystyle\tau\in(-\overline{\tau},+\overline{\tau})\text{ such that}

\displaystyle q=x(\tau)\text{ and }x_{\star}\in\partial\mathcal{S}_{\star}\cap\mathcal{U}_{\star}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\exists\,(x,\widehat{\beta})\text{ in the congruence and}

\displaystyle[\text{by }\eqref{eq:hyp_boundary}]

\displaystyle\tau\in(-\overline{\tau},+\overline{\tau})\text{ such that}

\displaystyle q=x(\tau)\text{ and }x_{\star}\in\partial\mathscr{I}\cap\mathcal{U}_{\star}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\exists\,(x,\widehat{\beta})\text{ in the congruence and}

\displaystyle[\text{by def. of }\mathscr{I}]

\displaystyle\tau\in(-\overline{\tau},+\overline{\tau})\text{ such that }q=x(\tau)

\displaystyle\text{and }\breve{\Theta}_{\star}(y^{1}(x_{\star}),y^{2}(x_{\star}),y^{3}(x_{\star}))=0

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\breve{\Theta}_{\star}(y^{1}(q),y^{2}(q),y^{3}(q))

\displaystyle[\text{by Gaussian coord.}]

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\breve{\Theta}(y^{0}(q),y^{1}(q),y^{2}(q),y^{3}(q))=0

\displaystyle[\text{by }\eqref{gauge_ineq},\eqref{gauge_IcapU}]

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle q\in\mathscr{I}\cap\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle[\text{by def. of }\mathscr{I}]\,.

Consequently, (
4.33d
) holds. ∎

4.2.3
Derivation of the evolution system

The gauge constructed in the section above leads to a convenient evolution system based on some of the frame components of the zero quantities defined by (
4.26
). Near the boundary, particular care must be paid to the treatment of the zero quantities
\varphi_{\bf abc}
, which lead to the evolution on the rescaled Weyl tensor, in order to prove the local existence and uniqueness of solutions for both the evolution system and the propagation of the constraints which will be studied in
Section

4.2.5
. More precisely, the evolution system must be designed so that the constraints propagate tangentially to the boundary. This ensures that no additional first-order boundary conditions are required. For a detailed explanation of this difficulty, one can refer to
[
78
, Example 32]
, which discusses a model equation, or
[
50
]
, which is set in the simpler framework of AdS space.

Lemma 4.28
.

Under the hypotheses of
4.27
and in the gauge constructed therein, the following differential equations on some frame components of the electromagnetic decomposition
(\breve{E},\breve{H})
of the Weyl candidate
\breve{V}
in the adapted frame
(e_{\bf 0},(e_{\bf i}))
hold on
\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}\left(\breve{E}_{\bf AB}\right)+\not{\epsilon}_{\bf(A|}{}^{\bf C}e_{\bf C}\left(\breve{H}_{\bf 3|B)}\right)-\not{\epsilon}_{\bf(A|}{}^{\bf C}e_{\bf 3}\left(\breve{H}_{\bf|B)C}\right)+N_{\bf AB}\left(\breve{E},\breve{H}\right)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.40a)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}\left(\breve{E}_{\bf 3A}\right)+\not{\epsilon}^{\bf BC}e_{\bf B}\left(\breve{H}_{\bf CA}\right)+N_{\bf A}\left(\breve{E},\breve{H}\right)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.40b)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}\left(\breve{H}_{\bf AB}\right)-\not{\epsilon}_{\bf(A|}{}^{\bf C}e_{\bf C}\left(\breve{E}_{\bf 3|B)}\right)+\not{\epsilon}_{\bf(A|}{}^{\bf C}e_{\bf 3}\left(\breve{E}_{\bf|B)C}\right)+N_{\bf AB}\left(\breve{H},-\breve{E}\right)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.40c)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}\left(\breve{H}_{\bf 3A}\right)-\not{\epsilon}^{\bf BC}e_{\bf B}\left(\breve{E}_{\bf CA}\right)+N_{\bf A}\left(\breve{H},-\breve{E}\right)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.40d)

where

\not{\epsilon}_{\bf AB}:=\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf 0AB3}\,,

and
N_{\bf AB}(X,Y)
,
N_{\bf A}(X,Y)
are linear terms in
(X,Y)
of the form

\widecheck{\Gamma}\times X+\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times X+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times Y+\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times Y\,,

with
N_{\bf AB}
symmetric.

Proof.

By
4.25

i)
, all zero quantities with
\acute{\nabla}=\widecheck{\nabla}
,
U_{\alpha\beta}=\widecheck{L}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\zeta_{\alpha}=(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\alpha}
and
s=\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}}
vanish identically. We will used here that
\varphi_{\bf abc}=0
for some frame indices and the electromagnetic decomposition
(\breve{E},\breve{H})
of the Weyl candidate
\breve{V}
.

•

Consider
\varphi_{\bf 0(AB)}=0
. Let us compute first
\varphi_{\bf 0AB}
. One has

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{\bf 0}\breve{V}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf B0A}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf 0}(\breve{V}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf B0A})=e_{\bf 0}(\breve{E}_{\bf AB})\,,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{\bf C}\breve{V}^{\bf C}{}_{\bf B0A}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf C}(\breve{V}^{\bf C}{}_{\bf B0A})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{V}=\not{\epsilon}_{\bf B}{}^{\bf C}e_{\bf C}(\breve{H}_{\bf 3A})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{E}+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{H}\,,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{\bf 3}\breve{V}^{\bf 3}{}_{\bf B0A}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf 3}(\breve{V}^{\bf 3}{}_{\bf B0A})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{V}=-\not{\epsilon}_{\bf B}{}^{\bf C}e_{\bf 3}(\breve{H}_{\bf AC})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{E}+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{H}\,,

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf a}\breve{V}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf A0B}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times\breve{V}=\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times\breve{E}+\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times\breve{H}\,.

Taking the symmetric part in
({\bf A},{\bf B})
gives (
4.40a
).

•

Consider
\varphi_{\bf 0A3}=0
. One has

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{\bf 0}\breve{V}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 30A}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf 0}(\breve{V}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 30A})=e_{\bf 0}(\breve{E}_{\bf 3A})\,,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{\bf B}\breve{V}^{\bf B}{}_{\bf 30A}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf B}(\breve{V}^{\bf B}{}_{\bf 30A})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{V}=\not{\epsilon}^{\bf BC}e_{\bf B}(\breve{H}_{\bf CA})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{E}+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{H}\,,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla}_{\bf 3}\breve{V}^{\bf 3}{}_{\bf 30A}

\displaystyle=e_{\bf 3}(\breve{V}^{\bf 3}{}_{\bf 30A})+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{V}=+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{E}+\widecheck{\Gamma}\times\breve{H}\,,

\displaystyle(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf a}\breve{V}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf 30A}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times\breve{V}=\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times\breve{E}+\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}}\times\breve{H}\,.

This gives (
4.40b
).

The other two equations are deduced by symmetry thanks to (
2.39
) and (
2.50
).
∎

Lemma 4.29
.

Under the hypotheses of
4.27
and in the gauge constructed therein, the following transport equations hold on
\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf k}{}_{\bf j})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{{}^{\bf l}}{}_{\bf 0}\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf l}{}^{\bf k}{}_{\bf j}

\displaystyle=\breve{\Theta}\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf j}{}^{\bf kl}\breve{H}_{\bf li}+\delta_{\bf j}{}^{\bf k}\widecheck{L}_{\bf i0}\,,

(4.41a)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 0})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{{}^{\bf l}}{}_{\bf 0}\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf l}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 0}

\displaystyle=\breve{\Theta}\,\delta^{\bf jk}\breve{E}_{\bf ik}+\delta^{\bf jk}\widecheck{L}_{\bf ik}\,,

(4.41b)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf j})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{{}^{\bf l}}{}_{\bf 0}\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf l}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf j}

\displaystyle=\breve{\Theta}\,\breve{E}_{\bf ij}+\widecheck{L}_{\bf ij}\,,

(4.41c)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 0})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 0}\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf j}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 0}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{L}_{\bf i0}\,,

(4.41d)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}((\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf i})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf j}

\displaystyle=\widecheck{L}_{\bf i0}\,,

(4.41e)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{L}_{\bf ij})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf k}{}_{\bf 0}\widecheck{L}_{\bf kj}

\displaystyle=\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf j}{}^{\bf kl}(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf k}\breve{H}_{\bf li}+(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}\breve{E}_{\bf ij}\,,

(4.41f)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(\widecheck{L}_{\bf i0})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf k}{}_{\bf 0}\widecheck{L}_{\bf k0}

\displaystyle=(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf l}\delta^{\bf lk}\breve{E}_{\bf ki}\,,

(4.41g)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}((\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf i})

\displaystyle=0\,,

(4.41h)

\displaystyle e_{\bf 0}(e_{\bf i}{}^{\mu})+\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 0}e_{\bf j}{}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=-\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 0}\delta_{\bf 0}{}^{\mu}\,,

(4.41i)

where
\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf ijk}:=\breve{\epsilon}_{\bf 0ijk}
and
(\breve{E},\breve{H})
is the decomposition of the Weyl candidate
\breve{V}
in the frame field
(e_{\bf 0},(e_{\bf i}))
which is adapted to
\mathcal{S}_{\star}
given by
2.62
.

Proof.

By
4.25

i)
, all the zero quantities for
\acute{\nabla}=\widecheck{\nabla}
,
U_{\alpha\beta}=\widecheck{L}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\zeta_{\alpha}=(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\alpha}
and
s=\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}}
vanish identically. In the gauge constructed in
4.27
, one has

\displaystyle\varpi^{\bf k}{}_{\bf j0i}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_a}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\varpi^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 00i}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_b}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\varpi^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf j0i}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_c}\,,

\displaystyle\varpi^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 00i}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_d}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\Pi_{\bf 0i}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_e}\,,

\displaystyle\;\varrho_{\bf 0ij}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_f}\,,

\displaystyle\varrho_{\bf 0i0}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_g}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\varkappa_{\bf 0i}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_h}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\widecheck{\Sigma}_{\bf 0}{}^{\bf a}{}_{\bf i}e_{\bf a}{}^{\mu}=0

\displaystyle\iff\eqref{transport_i}\,.

∎

Define the unknown
\underline{u}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
with
m:=90
by

\displaystyle\underline{u}:=\Big(\breve{E}_{\bf 11},\breve{E}_{\bf 22},\breve{H}_{\bf 12},\breve{H}_{\bf 21},\breve{H}_{\bf 11},\breve{H}_{\bf 22},\breve{E}_{\bf 12},\breve{E}_{\bf 21},\breve{E}_{\bf 31},\breve{E}_{\bf 32},\breve{H}_{\bf 31},\breve{H}_{\bf 32},

\displaystyle\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf k},\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf j}{}_{\bf 0},\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf j},\widecheck{\Gamma}_{\bf i}{}^{\bf 0}{}_{\bf 0},(\widecheck{\kappa}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf i},\widecheck{L}_{\bf ij},\widecheck{L}_{\bf i0},(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf i},e_{\bf i}{}^{\mu}\Big)\,.

The frame indices in the first line are written explicitly to specify their order in the tuple, as opposed to the second line. This function
\underline{u}
is a solution to the evolution system composed of equations (
4.40a
)-(
4.40d
) and (
4.41a
)-(
4.41i
) where
\widecheck{s}_{\breve{g}}
,
(\widecheck{\zeta}_{\breve{g}})_{\bf 0}
and
\breve{\Theta}
are given by (
4.33a
)-(
4.33c
).

The remaining part of this section is dedicated to writing the evolution system verified by
\underline{u}
outside of the geometric framework. Set

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{4}_{3\geq 0}

\displaystyle:=\{y=(y^{0},y^{1},y^{2},y^{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\mid y^{3}\geq 0\}\,,

\displaystyle\partial\mathbb{R}^{4}_{3\geq 0}

\displaystyle=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{4}_{3\geq 0}\,|\,y^{3}=0\}\,.

The open subset
\mathcal{U}_{\star}
is identified through the coordinates to an open subset of
